# Roth/HasseDeriv.lean / Roth/Index.lean

475 comparisons among the 293 accepted declarations. [Source hashes and review provenance](HasseDeriv--Index.json).

| Declaration A | Declaration B | Same statement? | Same proof / mathematical relationship? | Recommended action |

| `Roth.index` | `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff` | no | A: Defines the index as the minimum reciprocal-weighted exponent among nonzero Taylor coefficients, using real infimum with zero value at the zero polynomial. Mechanism: Takes sInf of the image of the finite support of the project taylor under weight. B: Defines the coordinatewise binomial coefficient for two finitely supported natural multi-indices, multiplying only over the differentiation index support. Mechanism: Uses Finsupp.prod with Nat.choose; omitted coordinates contribute choose(k,0)=1, so the finite-support presentation gives the full multivariate binomial factor. Hasse identities manipulate a chosen derivative and its coefficients, while index is a minimum over all nonzero translated coefficients. Choosing one nonzero derivative yields only an upper bound on that minimum; proving a lower bound requires all smaller-weight derivatives to vanish. Exact order composition and natural binomial factors underlie index loss, but neither define nor by themselves prove the extremal statement. Zero-polynomial index0 and positive-characteristic scalar vanishing prevent naive replacement of divided derivatives by ordinary derivative orders. | Retain operator laws separately from extremal-order consequences; share the explicit coefficient/composition/degree ingredients, with nonzero hypotheses and totalization preserved. |
